# Vulnerability ledger

One row per vulnerability class or advisory the SDK's protocols are exposed
to. Method: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-12-vuln-and-perf-audit-plan.md`.
Machine check: `python3 scripts/vulnaudit/ledger_check.py docs/security/vuln-ledger.md`.

Row grammar (one row = one entry, continuation lines indented):

```
- [ ] V-0001 <source id> (<product|RFC section>) <one-line class/summary>
      ours: <src path or n/a> — verdict: ?|fixed|already-safe|n/a — test: <name> — commit: <sha> — perf: <lane delta or n/a>
```

- `[ ]` untriaged, `[~]` triaged (verdict + planned test), `[x]` closed.
- A row may be `[x]` only with `verdict: fixed` + test + commit, or
  `verdict: already-safe` + test + commit (the pinning test), or
  `verdict: n/a` + a reason in place of the test.
- Update the row in the same commit that closes it.

## Summary

Phase 1 (2026-09-12): 836 rows from six raw sources (spec paragraphs 302 +
130 + 4, peer-implementation advisories 62 + 56 + 223, literature 41, mp4
parsers 22, npm audits 20; deduplicated by id/alias). Per-source coverage
tables live with the raw data (tasks/vuln/raw/*.coverage.md). Known gaps:
X.690 not directly fetchable (covered via RFC 5280 §8 / RFC 7468), ~35
Bouncy Castle 2026 advisories without fetched detail, NVD bulk queries
rate-limited (vendor pages and GitHub Advisory used instead).

Counts: `python3 scripts/vulnaudit/ledger_check.py docs/security/vuln-ledger.md`.

      ours: src/common/platform/qlog/qlogevent.c — verdict: n/a — test: Our qlog writer (qev_w) is a bounded cursor that poisons itself once out of capacity; ACK ranges reach it as already-validated ack_frame fields (ack.c's take_first/pair_fits), never re-derived from raw wire ints. ngtcp2's fixed-buffer qlog serializer class does not exist here. — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: Our qlog writer (qev_w) is a bounded cursor that poisons itself once out of capacity; ACK ranges reach it as already-validated ack_frame fields (ack.c's take_first/pair_fits), never re-derived from raw wire ints. ngtcp2's fixed-buffer qlog serializer class does not exist here.

      ours: src/tls/handshake/core/tls/hsdriver.c:hsd_auth_gate_ok — verdict: already-safe — test: test_server_finished_rejected_before_auth — commit: existing — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: hsdriver's flight table requires HSD_CERTIFICATE then HSD_CERT_VERIFY before HSD_FINISHED can even be offered (hsd_table_match forbids forward jumps) and hsd_auth_gate_ok additionally refuses HSD_FINISHED unless cert_verified was set by hsdriver_cert_verified, so Finished without a verified Certificate/CertificateVerify is structurally rejected; there is also no post-handshake (session-ticket-triggered re-auth) code path in this SDK at all.
- [x] V-0242 CVE-2026-11703 (wolfssl) key-schedule: Missing SNI/ALPN binding on stateful session resumption.
      ours: src/tls/handshake/flight/resume/resume.c + src/tls/keys/ticket/ticket.c — verdict: fixed — test: test_resume_rejects_alpn_sni_mismatch (client-side cache; server ticket carries no ALPN yet — follow-up noted in F9-report.md) — commit: 4bc6edb0 — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: The sealed ticket (ticket_encode/ticket_decode) only carries secret/issued_at/lifetime/age_add; it binds no SNI or ALPN value, so a resumed session's SNI/ALPN are renegotiated fresh each time with no crosscheck that they match the original session's identity, allowing an established PSK to be reused under a different ALPN/SNI than it was issued for.

      ours: src/transport/conn/loop/connrunner/keyupdate.c:connrunner_maybe_initiate_ku; src/tls/keys/keyupdate/aeadlimit.c:aead_needs_update — verdict: fixed — test: test_srvloop_send_below_aead_limit_keeps_generation, test_srvloop_send_at_aead_limit_initiates_key_update, test_srvloop_no_second_update_before_peer_follows — commit: 675ae47a — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: aead_needs_update (RFC 9001 6.6 confidentiality limit, 2^23 packets for AES-GCM) is defined and unit-tested but never called from src/ production code (grep confirms zero non-test callers); connrunner_maybe_initiate_ku, which would proactively rotate keys before the nonce/AEAD-invocation limit is reached, is likewise never invoked from the connection send loop (only aead_integrity_exceeded, the auth-failure/decrypt-limit check, is wired into connloop.c). A connection that sends packets fast enough could reach the AES-GCM 2^23-packet confidentiality limit without a self-initiated key update ever firing.

      ours: src/transport/packet/protect/protect_suite/aead_suite.c:suite_nonce — verdict: already-safe — test: test_aead_suite_nonce — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: Nonce is deterministically iv xor pn per RFC 9001 5.3 (never independently random), and each 1-RTT/0-RTT/handshake key phase has its own IV derived via HKDF-Expand-Label, so nonce reuse under one key requires packet-number reuse, which QUIC's monotonic PN space and the (unwired, see V-0763) confidentiality-limit mechanism are meant to prevent -- the construction itself is correct, but see V-0763 for the missing enforcement of the packet-count ceiling.
- [x] V-0765 L-0035 (Poly1305 one-time-key reuse en) key-reuse-mac-forgery: 
      ours: src/crypto/symmetric/aead/chacha/aead.c:poly_key — verdict: already-safe — test: test_aead — commit: existing — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: The Poly1305 one-time key is derived fresh per invocation from chacha20_block(key, counter=0, nonce, ...) -- i.e. from the per-packet AEAD nonce (itself derived from iv xor pn, RFC 9001 5.3) -- so a new Poly1305 key is used for every packet and is never reused across two different messages under the same connection key.

      ours: src/transport/conn/loop/connrunner/keyupdate.c:may_initiate; src/tls/keys/keyupdate/aeadlimit.c:aead_needs_update — verdict: fixed — test: test_srvloop_recv_auth_failure_counts_toward_integrity_limit, test_srvrun_closes_with_aead_limit_reached_on_step, test_srvloop_send_at_aead_limit_initiates_key_update — commit: c375f167 — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: Same root cause as V-0763: the confidentiality-limit-driven key-update path is unwired from the send loop, so the RFC 8439/9001 requirement that nonce uniqueness be enforced before the AEAD's packet limit is reached has no live enforcement path in production; nonce construction itself (iv xor pn) is correct but nothing forces a rotation before exhaustion.

      ours: n/a — verdict: n/a — test: wired's Poly1305 key is not independently randomly generated -- it is derived deterministically via chacha20_block(key, 0, nonce) from the connection's already-established AEAD key/nonce (RFC 8439 2.6), so 'weak RNG for the Poly1305 key' does not apply; the security of the derivation instead rests on ChaCha20's own PRF security, already covered by V-0768. — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: wired's Poly1305 key is not independently randomly generated -- it is derived deterministically via chacha20_block(key, 0, nonce) from the connection's already-established AEAD key/nonce (RFC 8439 2.6), so 'weak RNG for the Poly1305 key' does not apply; the security of the derivation instead rests on ChaCha20's own PRF security, already covered by V-0768.

      ours: src/crypto/asymmetric/ecc/p256sign/rfc6979.c,ed25519_sign.c — verdict: already-safe — test: test_rfc6979_sample_k, test_ed25519_sign_test1 — commit: existing — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: Both ECDSA (RFC 6979 HMAC-DRBG) and EdDSA (SHA-512-derived r) nonce generation are deterministic given (private key, message), matching this row's stated benefit: they are directly testable against fixed test vectors, which test_rfc6979_sample_k and test_ed25519_sign_test1..3 (RFC 8032 vectors) both do.

      ours: src/crypto/asymmetric/ecc/p256sign/rfc6979.c:ps_mix,ps_advance — verdict: already-safe — test: test_rfc6979_sample_k — commit: existing — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: The nonce derivation uses HMAC-SHA256 (hmac_sha256) throughout, which is the standard HMAC construction proven a secure PRF under standard assumptions; this SDK does not substitute a weaker MAC or a raw hash in the RFC 6979 construction.
- [x] V-0750 S-6979-4-5 (RFC 6979) Deterministic nonce derivation as a side-channel target: The determinism of the algorithms described in this note may be useful to an attacker in s
      ours: src/crypto/asymmetric/ecc/p256sign/rfc6979.c — verdict: already-safe — test: test_rfc6979_retry_skips_rejected_candidates — commit: existing — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: Deterministic nonce derivation here has no data-dependent branch/table-index on the private key or message (fixed-size HMAC calls only, per-candidate cost identical regardless of whether the candidate is accepted or rejected except for the accept/reject decision itself, which is public-shape: the number of retries is astronomically unlikely to ever exceed 1 and does not correlate observably with any single key bit).
- [x] V-0751 S-6979-4-6 (RFC 6979) Private key used both for signing and as HMAC_DRBG input requires HMAC to remain secure under key exposure conditions: The scheme requires HMAC to remain a random oracle/secure PRF even in this dual-use config

## WebTransport (draft-ietf-webtrans-http3)

- [~] V-0492 CVE-2026-21434 (webtransport-go (WebTransport)) resource-exhaustion: Memory exhaustion via oversized WT_CLOSE_SESSION Application Error Message: the draft mandates a 1024-byte lim
      ours: src/app/webtransport/capsule/wtcapsule/wtcapsule.c:wired_wtcapsule_encode_close,wired_wtcapsule_decode_close — verdict: already-safe — test: (claimed test_wtcapsule_close_message_max_len: not in tests/; pinning test needed) — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: Both encode and decode enforce the draft's WTCAPSULE_CLOSE_MESSAGE_MAX (1024-byte) cap on the WT_CLOSE_SESSION Application Error Message: encode rejects (`return 0`) a message longer than the cap before writing anything, and decode's wtcapsule_is_close rejects any value whose message portion exceeds the cap, so an oversized field can neither be produced nor accepted by this SDK's own capsule codec.
- [x] V-0493 CVE-2026-21435 (webtransport-go (WebTransport)) resource-exhaustion: Indefinite session-close hang: a malicious peer can withhold QUIC flow-control credit on the CONNECT stream, b
      ours: src/app/http3/server/srvrun/srvrun.c (WT session close path) — verdict: n/a — test: This CVE is about a client-library hang waiting for QUIC flow-control credit specifically on the CONNECT stream during session close; wired is the server-side SDK and its close path (wt_close_pending/wt_close_msg buffered per session, srvrun.c) drives sends from its own event loop rather than blocking synchronously on peer-controlled credit, so the specific indefinite-block failure mode described does not have a matching code path to assess as vulnerable here. — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: This CVE is about a client-library hang waiting for QUIC flow-control credit specifically on the CONNECT stream during session close; wired is the server-side SDK and its close path (wt_close_pending/wt_close_msg buffered per session, srvrun.c) drives sends from its own event loop rather than blocking synchronously on peer-controlled credit, so the specific indefinite-block failure mode described does not have a matching code path to assess as vulnerable here.
- [~] V-0494 CVE-2026-21438 (webtransport-go (WebTransport)) resource-exhaustion: Unbounded memory consumption via repeated creation and closing of many WebTransport streams: no limit on the r
      ours: src/app/http3/server/srvrun/srvrun.c:SRVRUN_MAX_WT_SESSIONS (=2); src/app/webtransport/session/session/session.c:wired_wt_session_offer_stream — verdict: already-safe — test: test_srvrun_wt_stream_open_allowed_bounded — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: Concurrent WebTransport sessions per connection are hard-capped at SRVRUN_MAX_WT_SESSIONS==2 (srvrun.c:169), and within a session, buffered (pre-established) streams are capped at WIRED_WT_MAX_BUFFERED_STREAMS via stream_free_slot returning 0 once full (session.c:69-73), bounding memory from repeated stream churn; however, once a session is ESTABLISHED, wired_wt_session_stream_open_allowed only rejects based on max_streams_bidi/uni, which stays 0 (unlimited) forever because no capsule ever calls set_max_streams (see V-0503/V-0504) -- so post-establishment stream creation IS effectively unbounded by any peer-negotiated ceiling, only by the SDK's own local caller-set value.
- [~] V-0495 CVE-2026-57497 (webtransport-go (WebTransport, capsule parser)) resource-exhaustion: Memory exhaustion via unknown capsule buffering: when skipping an unrecognized WebTransport capsule type, the 
      ours: src/app/http3/core/capsule/capsule.c:capsule_decode — verdict: already-safe — test: test_capsule_decode_view_no_alloc — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: capsule_decode never allocates or buffers based on the declared Capsule Length; it only returns a wired_span VIEW into the caller-owned input buffer after checking `len <= data.n - value_start`, so an unrecognized/unknown capsule type cannot cause memory exhaustion in this parser -- there is no NSS-style incremental-buffer-growth behavior at all. (Note: no caller in src/ currently invokes capsule_decode on the WT CONNECT-stream receive path -- see V-0503-family gaps -- so this defense, while structurally sound, has no live receive path exercising it yet.)
- [~] V-0496 L-0038 (WebTransport session-close DoS) flow-control-blocking: 
      ours: src/app/http3/server/srvrun/srvrun.c (no CONNECT-stream capsule receive loop) — verdict: needs-fix — test: test_srvrun_wt_close_session_capsule_received — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: The session-close DoS class (peer withholds flow control while the SDK waits on a close handshake) cannot be evaluated as defended because there is no code path that reads/decodes session-level capsules (WT_CLOSE_SESSION, DRAIN) arriving from the peer on the CONNECT stream at all -- srvrun.c only encodes and sends these capsules (wired_wtcapsule_encode_close, wtcapsule_encode_drain), it never calls wired_wtcapsule_decode_close or wtcapsule_decode_drain. A malicious peer's close-capsule behavior is therefore neither parsed nor bounded on receive.
- [~] V-0497 L-0039 (WebTransport WT_CLOSE_SESSION ) unbounded-length-allocation: 
      ours: src/app/webtransport/capsule/wtcapsule/wtcapsule.c:wired_wtcapsule_decode_close — verdict: already-safe — test: (claimed test_wtcapsule_decode_close_rejects_oversized: not in tests/; pinning test needed) — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: wtcapsule_is_close rejects (returns 0, no allocation/copy) any WT_CLOSE_SESSION value whose message length exceeds WTCAPSULE_CLOSE_MESSAGE_MAX (1024) or is shorter than the 4-byte error code, before wtcapsule_take_close ever runs, so decode itself cannot be driven into an oversized allocation even though (per V-0496) nothing in src/ currently calls this decoder on the receive path.
- [~] V-0498 L-0040 (WebTransport unknown-capsule b) unbounded-length-allocation: 
      ours: src/app/http3/core/capsule/capsule.c:capsule_decode — verdict: already-safe — test: test_capsule_decode_view_no_alloc — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: Same view-not-copy behavior as V-0495: an unknown/unsupported capsule type decoded via capsule_decode never triggers buffering proportional to its declared length -- callers receive a bounded span or a decode failure, never an allocation sized by attacker-controlled length.

      ours: n/a — verdict: n/a — test: TLS client-certificate-based peer authentication is a MAY-level optional capability of the overview draft; wired's TLS stack supports the standard handshake but no code in src/app/webtransport exposes or requires client-cert auth as part of WebTransport session establishment, so there's no specific WT-layer defense to assess -- this is core TLS scope, not WebTransport-specific. — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: TLS client-certificate-based peer authentication is a MAY-level optional capability of the overview draft; wired's TLS stack supports the standard handshake but no code in src/app/webtransport exposes or requires client-cert auth as part of WebTransport session establishment, so there's no specific WT-layer defense to assess -- this is core TLS scope, not WebTransport-specific.
- [~] V-0503 S-webtrans-h3-5.1-1 (draft-ietf-webtrans-http3-15) flow control MUST be enabled when sessions share a connection: a WebTransport endpoint that allows a session to share an underlying transport connection 
      ours: src/app/http3/server/srvrun/srvrun.c; src/app/webtransport/session/session/session.c:wired_wt_session_data_send_allowed,stream_open_allowed — verdict: needs-fix — test: test_srvrun_wt_session_sharing_enables_flow_control — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: Sessions in this SDK always share the single underlying HTTP/3 connection (SRVRUN_MAX_WT_SESSIONS==2 sessions multiplexed on one QUIC connection), which per the draft means flow control MUST be enabled -- but no code path ever calls wired_wt_session_set_max_data/set_max_streams from a received capsule or from session establishment, so max_data/max_streams_bidi/uni stay at their init value of 0, and the accompanying accessor functions treat 0 as 'unlimited' (limit == 0 || ...). Flow control is therefore never actually enabled for shared sessions, violating the MUST.

- [~] V-0505 S-webtrans-h3-5.1-3 (draft-ietf-webtrans-http3-15) stale flow-control capsules must be ignored when flow control is not enabled: if flow control is not enabled, an endpoint MUST ignore receipt of any flow control capsul
      ours: src/app/http3/server/srvrun/srvrun.c (no capsule receive/dispatch for flow-control capsules) — verdict: needs-fix — test: test_srvrun_wt_ignores_stale_flow_control_capsules — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P2 — why: The 'MUST ignore stale flow-control capsules when flow control is not enabled' requirement cannot be honored deliberately because no code path decodes incoming MAX_STREAMS/MAX_DATA/STREAMS_BLOCKED/DATA_BLOCKED capsules at all (wtcapsule_decode_max_data/decode_max_streams/decode_streams_blocked/decode_data_blocked have zero non-test callers in src/) -- the current behavior is 'never look at them' rather than 'look, then intentionally discard while disabled', which happens to have the same externally-observable effect today but leaves no code path ready for when flow control IS enabled per V-0503's fix.

      ours: src/app/webtransport/session/session/session.c:wired_wt_session_set_max_data,set_max_streams (never called from src/ production code) — verdict: needs-fix — test: test_srvrun_wt_session_sharing_enables_flow_control — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: The draft's MUST ('a WebTransport endpoint MUST implement flow control mechanisms if it allows session sharing') is not met in practice: the flow-control primitives exist and are unit-tested in isolation, but no production caller ever applies a peer-advertised or locally-decided non-zero limit, so pooled/shared sessions on one connection have no enforced resource ceiling from the WT layer (same root cause as V-0503).

      ours: src/app/http3/server/srvrun/srvrun.c (no feature-usage counters/telemetry for WT streams/sessions) — verdict: needs-fix — test: test_srvrun_wt_usage_counters_exposed — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P3 — why: No monitoring/counting of WebTransport feature usage (concurrent sessions, incoming bidi/uni stream counts, datagram rate) is exposed by srvrun.c beyond the fixed structural caps (SRVRUN_MAX_WT_SESSIONS, buffered-stream/datagram slot arrays); the SHOULD is about operational visibility for anomaly detection, which is absent -- though this is a hardening/observability gap rather than a directly exploitable one.

## IP / UDP / AF_XDP I/O

- [~] V-0489 GHSA-px8v-pp82-rcvr (quic-go) path-mtu-spoofing: On Linux, an off-path attacker can inject spoofed ICMP 'Packet Too Big' / Fragmentation Needed messages to for
      ours: src/transport/conn/cid/pmtu/pmtu.c:pmtu_next_probe/pmtu_on_ack/pmtu_on_loss + connrunner/pmtudrive.c — verdict: already-safe — test: (claimed test_pmtu_reconcile_ignores_unmatched_probe: not in tests/; pinning test needed) — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: This SDK implements RFC 8899 DPLPMTUD entirely at the QUIC layer (PING+PADDING probes acked/lost via QUIC-level ACK reconciliation, connrunner_pmtu_reconcile matching only the connection's own outstanding probe pn) and never reads or trusts any raw ICMP message for PMTU updates -- no ICMP socket, IP_RECVERR/MSG_ERRQUEUE, or sock_extended_err handling exists anywhere in src/ (grep confirmed empty). An off-path spoofed ICMP message has no code path into this PMTU state machine at all.
- [~] V-0490 GHSA-hxq4-mx37-fqvg (s2n-quic) nil-deref/crash-on-frame: Potential denial-of-service vulnerability in s2n-quic when receiving empty UDP packets.
      ours: src/transport/io/udp/udploop/rxloop.c:udploop_rx — verdict: already-safe — test: test_udploop_rx_zero_length_datagram_no_crash — commit:  — perf: — layer: unit P1 — why: udploop_rx treats any recv() return `<= 0` (including a legitimate zero-length UDP datagram) as EAGAIN/empty/error and returns 0 immediately without touching buf or calling udploop_split, so a zero-length datagram cannot reach frame/coalesce parsing at all.
- [~] V-0491 CVE-2024-2169 (UDP application-layer protocol implementations generally (Loop DoS)) loop-dos: Loop DoS: crafted UDP packets with a spoofed source address can cause two servers running UDP-based applicatio
      ours: src/transport/conn/cid/sreset/sreset.c:sreset_size + srvrun.c:srvrun_sreset_applies — verdict: already-safe — test: (claimed test_sreset_size_strictly_below_trigger_len: not in tests/; pinning test needed) — commit:  — perf: — layer: tla P1 — why: Stateless reset (this SDK's only unauthenticated UDP-triggered reply) follows RFC 9000 10.3.3's own loop-prevention rule exactly: srvrun_sreset_applies refuses to reply to an Initial-shaped or undersized (<= SRESET_MIN) datagram, and sreset_size/srvrun_seal_stateless_reset cap the reply strictly below the triggering datagram's length (`cap = trigger_len - 1`), so a reset can never itself trigger a same-or-larger reply forming CVE-2024-2169's amplifying loop. VERSION_NEGOTIATION (vneg_build) is likewise a bounded one-shot reply with no server-side path that reacts to receiving another VN/error-shaped packet by sending one back.

- [x] V-0824 L-mp4-0001 (ISO/IEC 14496-12 box size=0/si) box-length-overflow: 
      ours: src/app/media/mp4frag/mp4frag.c:mp4frag_size_ok / mp4frag_box_take — verdict: already-safe — test: test_mp4frag_scan_rejects_malformed (largesize / to-end-of-file cases) — commit: existing — perf: — layer: fuzz P2 — why: size==0 (extend-to-EOF) and size==1 (64-bit largesize) are both explicitly refused: mp4frag_size_ok requires 8 <= size <= remaining, so 0 and 1 both fail that lower bound and mp4frag_box_take returns 0 immediately -- neither hazard case is specially interpreted, matching mp4frag.h's documented refusal of both.
- [x] V-0825 L-mp4-0002 (ISO/IEC 14496-12 nested box le) box-length-overflow: 
      ours: src/app/media/mp4frag/mp4frag.c:mp4frag_size_ok — verdict: already-safe — test: test_mp4frag_scan_rejects_malformed (mdat size past the end) — commit: existing — perf: — layer: fuzz P2 — why: Every box's declared size is checked against size <= remaining (the actual bytes left in the buffer, i.e. the true containing scope) before *off advances, so a child box size cannot exceed what is actually present -- there is no separate parent/child nesting to overflow since mp4frag.c only walks top-level boxes (ftyp/moov opaque, moof+mdat pairs), but the same remaining-bytes bound applies at every step.
- [x] V-0826 L-mp4-0003 (ISO/IEC 14496-12 huge sample/e) box-length-overflow: 
      ours: src/app/media/mp4frag/mp4frag.h:MP4FRAG_MAX_FRAGS — verdict: already-safe — test: test_mp4frag_scan_rejects_too_many — commit: existing — perf: — layer: fuzz P2 — why: mp4frag has no sample-table entry counts to trust in the first place (mp4frag.h: no sample-table parsing) and its own fragment table is a fixed-capacity array (MP4FRAG_MAX_FRAGS=64) that mp4frag_mdat_ok refuses to grow past, so there is no unbounded allocation driven by any attacker-controlled count.
- [x] V-0827 L-mp4-0004 (ISO/IEC 14496-12 fragmented-fi) box-length-overflow: 
      ours: src/app/media/mp4frag/mp4frag.c:mp4frag_take_mdat / mp4frag_box_take — verdict: already-safe — test: test_mp4frag_scan_two_fragments / test_mp4frag_scan_rejects_malformed — commit: existing — perf: — layer: fuzz P2 — why: This record's own attack text names app/media/mp4frag directly; mp4frag_take_mdat computes each fragment span as moof.start..mdat.end using mdat.end derived from mp4frag_box_take's already-bounds-checked size (<= remaining), so a fragment span can never point past the file's actual bytes -- there is no separate trun/tfhd sample-offset computation in this SDK to go wrong, because no sample tables are parsed (mp4frag hands back only the whole moof+mdat span, not per-sample byte ranges).
